\textstyle{(?,P_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(?,P_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(?,\tau_{n}^{-1}X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(?,P_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(?,P_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(?,P_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Thus, the object
\Sigma^{n+1}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}})(X^{\wedge})
is quasi-isomorphic to
\tau_{n}^{-1}(X)^{\wedge}
. ∎

Corollary 8.7
.

Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{proj}B_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{mod}B_{0}
be the subcategory of projectives and let
\mathcal{A}
be a subcategory of
\mathrm{mod}B_{0}
which contains
\mathcal{B}
as a full subcategory. Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is homologically smooth and is
n
-rigid in
\mathrm{mod}B
. Then the relative
(n+2)
-Calabi–Yau completion of
f_{0}\colon\mathcal{B}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}

f\colon\bm{\Pi}_{n+1}(\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})

can be described as follows:

•

The objects in
\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
are the same as those of
{\mathcal{A}}
;

•

For any two objects
L
,
M
in
\mathcal{A}
, the space
\mathrm{Hom}_{\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})}(L,M)
is given by

\begin{split}\mathrm{Hom}_{\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})}(L,M)&\cong\mathrm{\mathbf{R}Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(L^{\wedge},\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}M^{\wedge}\overset{\rm\textbf{L}}{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Sigma^{n+1}\Theta_{f_{0}})^{\overset{\rm\textbf{L}}{\otimes}_{i}})\\
&\cong\mathrm{\mathbf{R}Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(L^{\wedge},\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}(\tau_{n}^{-i}M)^{\wedge})\\
&\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{B_{0}}(L,\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}(\tau_{n}^{-i}M)).\end{split}

In particular, the dg category
\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
is concentrated in degree 0 and we have a fully faithful functor

H^{0}(f)\colon H^{0}(\bm{\Pi}_{n+1}(\mathcal{B}))\cong\widetilde{B_{0}}\hookrightarrow\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}).

8.2.
n
-representation-finite algebras

Let
n\geqslant 0
be an integer. Let
B_{0}
be a finite dimensional algebra with global dimension at most
n
.

Definition 8.8
.

[
26
]

We say that
B_{0}
is
\tau_{n}
-finite
if
\tau_{n}^{-i}B_{0}=0
for sufficiently large
i
. We say that
B_{0}
is
n
-representation-finite
if
\mathrm{mod}B_{0}
contains an
n
-cluster tilting object.

Remark 8.9
.

If
B_{0}
is
n
-representation-finite, then it is
\tau_{n}
-finite.

Theorem 8.10
.

[
26
, Proposition 1.3]

Suppose that
B_{0}
is an
n
-representation-finite algebra. Then

\widetilde{B_{0}}\cong\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\tau_{B_{0},n}^{-i}B_{0}
is the unique basic
n
-cluster tilting object in
\mathrm{mod}B_{0}
.

Theorem 8.11
.

[
25
, Theorem 0.2]

Let
B_{0}
be
n
-representation-finite. Then

\mathrm{gldim}\,\mathrm{End}_{B_{0}}(\widetilde{B_{0}})\leqslant n+1.

Now we assume that
B_{0}
is
n
-representation-finite. The corresponding
n
-Auslander algebra
is given by
\mathrm{End}_{B_{0}}(\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\tau_{n}^{-i}B_{0})
. We denote it by
A_{0}
. Then there is a natural fully faithful morphism

\textstyle{f_{0}\colon B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{0}=\mathrm{End}_{B_{0}}(\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\tau_{n}^{-i}B_{0}).}

Lemma 8.12
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg category. Let
\mathcal{P}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
be two full dg subcategories such that
\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{A})=\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{P})\sqcup\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{B})
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(B,P)
is acyclic for all
B\in\mathcal{B}
,
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Then the Drinfeld dg quotient
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
is Morita equivalent to
\mathcal{B}
.

Proof
.
The restriction functor
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
induced by
f\colon\mathcal{B}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}
is a localization functor. Moreover, its kernel is generated (as a localizing subcategory) by its intersection with
\mathrm{per}\mathcal{A}
. Since the space
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(B,P)
is acyclic for all
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
P\in\mathcal{P}
, the induction functor

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{P})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

induces an equivalence between
\ker(f_{*})
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{P})
. Thus, we have an exact sequence of triangulated categories

0\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow 0.

It follows that the Drinfeld dg quotient
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
is Morita equivalent to
\mathcal{B}
.

∎

Proposition 8.13
.

Let
e=f_{0}(\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}})
. The homotopy cofiber of
f_{0}\colon B_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}
is equal to the usual quotient
A_{0}/A_{0}eA_{0}
, i.e. the
stable Auslander algebra
of
B_{0}
.

Proof
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{B}
be the following full subcategories of
\mathrm{mod}B_{0}

\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{ind}(\mathrm{add}(\{\tau_{n}^{-i}(B_{0})\ |\ i\geqslant 0\})),

\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{ind}(\mathrm{proj}B_{0}),

\mathcal{B}=\{M\in\mathcal{A}\ |\ M\notin\mathcal{P}\}.

In particular, if
n=0
then
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{P}}
.

For
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
M\in\mathcal{B}
, we have

\begin{split}D\mathrm{Hom}_{B_{0}}(M,P)\cong&\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(B_{0})}(P,\mathbb{S}_{B_{0}}(M))\\
=&\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(B_{0})}(P,\Sigma^{n}(\tau_{n}(M))).\end{split}

The above space vanishes since
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
\tau_{n}(M)\in\mathrm{mod}B_{0}
. Then, by Lemma
8.12
above, the homotopy cofiber of
f_{0}\colon B_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}
is equal to the usual quotient
A_{0}/A_{0}eA_{0}
.

∎

Proposition 8.14
.

Via the relative
(n+2)
-Calabi–Yau completion of
f_{0}\colon B_{0}\hookrightarrow A_{0}
, we get the following dg functor which has a canonical left
(n+2)
-Calabi–Yau structure

f\colon B=\bm{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})\longrightarrow A=\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0}).

Then

1)

The dg algebra
A
is concentrated in degree 0.

2)

H^{0}(A)
is a finite-dimensional algebra with finite global dimension at most
n+2
.

3)

The homotopy cofiber of
f
is equal to
\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0}/A_{0}eA_{0})
where
e=f(\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}})
.

4)

The functor
H^{0}(f)\colon H^{0}(B)=\widetilde{B_{0}}\to H^{0}(A)
is fully faithful.

5)

A
is internally bimodule
(n+2)
-Calabi–Yau with respect to
e=f(\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}})
.

Proof
.
The first and fourth statement follows from Corollary
8.7
. The third statement follows from Proposition
3.19
and the last statement follows from Proposition
6.6
. It remains to show the second one.

By Corollary
8.7
and the fact that
B_{0}
is
\tau_{n}
-finite, the algebra
H^{0}(\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0}))
is finite-dimensional. By Proposition
6.1
, the algebra
H^{0}(\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0}))
has finite global dimension at most
n+2
.
∎

We give an example where the relative Ginzburg algebra is not concentrated in degree 0.

Example 8.15
.

Let
(Q,F)
be the following ice quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3,}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

where the ice part
F
is give by the blue vertices and blue arrows. The underlying graded quiver of the corresponding relative Ginzburg algebra
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{rel}(Q,F)
is given as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
,
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{3}}

where
|b^{*}|=-1
and
|t_{3}|=-2
. The differential
d
takes the following values

d(a)=0=d(b),\quad d(b^{*})=0,\quad d(t_{3})=-b^{*}b.

It is easy to see that
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{rel}(Q,F)
is not concentrated in degree 0. In fact, the ice quiver
(Q,F)
is the mutation (see
[
61
]
) of the following ice quiver with potential
(Q^{\prime},F^{\prime},W^{\prime})
at the frozen vertex
2

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\boxed{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}

where the ice part
F^{\prime}
is given by the blue vertices and arrows and the potential
W^{\prime}
is
c^{\prime}b^{\prime}a^{\prime}
. However, the relative Ginzburg algebra
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{rel}(Q^{\prime},F^{\prime},W^{\prime})
associated with
(Q^{\prime},F^{\prime},W^{\prime})
is concentrated in degree
0
.

Suppose that
\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}}
has decomposition

\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}}=e_{1}+e_{2}+\cdots+e_{n}

into primitive orthogonal idempotents. We denote by
P_{i}=e_{i}B_{0}
the projective
B_{0}
-module associated with the idempotent
e_{i}
. Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be the following full subcategory of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})
(see
[
27
, Theorem 2.16]
)

{\mathcal{U}}=\mathrm{add}\{\mathbb{S}_{n}^{i}B_{0}\,|\,i\in\mathbb{Z}\}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0}),

where
\mathbb{S}_{n}=\Sigma^{-n}\mathbb{S}_{B_{0}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{B_{0}}=?\overset{\rm\textbf{L}}{\otimes}_{B_{0}}B_{0}^{\vee}
is the Serre functor of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})
. By
[
27
, Theorem 2.16]
,
{\mathcal{U}}
is an
n
-cluster tilting subcategory of
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})
.

Let
\Sigma^{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathcal{U}}=\mathrm{add}\{\Sigma^{p}\mathbb{S}_{n}^{k}B_{0}\,|\,p,k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
be the
\Sigma
closure of
{\mathcal{U}}
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})
. It is a bigraded category where the gradings are given by
\mathbb{S}_{n}
and
\Sigma
.

The dg algebra

\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})=T_{B_{0}}(\Sigma^{n}B_{0}^{\vee})

is Adams graded with
|\Sigma^{n}B_{0}^{\vee}|_{a}=1
. Then the homology algebra
H^{*}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
is naturally bigraded. We write
H^{p}_{k}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
for the homogeneous component in bidegree
(k,p)
, where
k
is the Adams degree and
p
is the cohomological degree. We denote by
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}=e_{i}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})
the cofibrant dg
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})
-module associated with
e_{i}
.

By the definition of
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})
, for each
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}
,
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{j}
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))}(P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i},\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{j})&=\bigoplus_{k\geqslant 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B_{0})}(P_{i},\Sigma^{p}P_{j}\overset{\rm\textbf{L}}{\otimes}_{B_{0}}(\Sigma^{n}B_{0}^{\vee})^{\overset{\rm\textbf{L}}{\otimes}_{k}})\\
&=\bigoplus_{k\geqslant 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B_{0})}(P_{i},\mathbb{S}_{n}^{-k}(\Sigma^{p}P_{j})).\end{split}

On the other hand, the space
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))}(P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i},\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{j})
is computed by
e_{j}H^{p}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))e_{i}
.

For any integer
k
, let
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}\langle k\rangle
be the shift of
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}
by degree
k
with respect to the Adams grading. We say a dg
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})
-module
M
has
bidegree
(k,p)
if it has Adams degree
k
and cohomological degree
p
. Denote by
{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
the category of Adams graded dg
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})
-modules with morphisms of bidegree
(0,0)
. The corresponding derived category is denoted by
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
. We denote by
T
the action of
\langle 1\rangle
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
.

For any two objects
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}
,
P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{j}
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})}(P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i},\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{j}\langle k\rangle)&\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})}(P_{i},\mathbb{S}_{n}^{-k}\Sigma^{p}P_{j})\\
&\cong e_{j}H^{p}_{k}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))e_{i}.\end{split}

We have an equivalence of bigraded categories

\mathrm{add}\{\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}\langle k\rangle\,|\,p,k\in\mathbb{Z}\}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\Sigma^{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathcal{U}}

which maps
\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}\langle k\rangle\
to
\mathbb{S}_{n}^{-k}\Sigma^{p}P_{i}
.

Via taking the orbit categories with respect to
T
and
\mathbb{S}_{n}
respectively, we have the following equivalence of graded categories

\mathrm{add}(\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}\langle k\rangle\\
,|\,i,k\in\mathbb{Z})/T\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\Sigma^{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathcal{U}}/\mathbb{S}_{n}.

We denote by
\mathrm{proj}^{\mathbb{Z}}(H^{*}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
the category of (cohomological) graded projective modules over
H^{*}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
. There is an equivalence of graded categories

\mathrm{add}(\Sigma^{p}P^{\mathbf{\Pi}}_{i}\langle k\rangle|\,i,k\in\mathbb{Z})/T\simeq\mathrm{proj}^{\mathbb{Z}}(H^{*}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})).

Thus we have an equivalence of graded categories

\mathrm{proj}^{\mathbb{Z}}(H^{*}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))\simeq\Sigma^{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathcal{U}}/\mathbb{S}_{n}.

Since
B_{0}
is
n
-representation-finite, by
[
27
, Theorem 3.1 and Proposition 3.6]
we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{mod}B_{0})}(\Sigma^{i}{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{U}})=0

for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
. By the above equivalence of graded categories, the
i
-th homology
H^{i}(\bm{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0}))
vanishes for
i=-1,\ldots,-n+1
.

Lemma 8.16
.

The higher preprojective algebra
\widetilde{B_{0}}
is self-injective.

Proof
.
It is enough to show that
\widetilde{B_{0}}=H^{0}(B)
is injective as a right
\widetilde{B_{0}}
-module. The category
\mathrm{pvd}(B)
has a canonical
t
-structure

(\mathrm{pvd}(B)_{\leqslant 0},\mathrm{pvd}(B)_{\geqslant 0}),

where
\mathrm{pvd}(B)_{\leqslant 0}
is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{pvd}(B)
whose objects are the dg modules
X
such that
H^{p}(X)
vanishes for all
p>0
and
\mathrm{pvd}(B)_{\geqslant 0}
is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{pvd}(B)
whose objects are the dg modules
X
such that
H^{p}(X)
vanishes for all
p<0
. The corresponding heart is equivalent to
\mathrm{mod}\widetilde{B_{0}}
. Moreover, by Section 3.1.7 of
[
5
]
, for all
X
and
Y
in
\mathrm{mod}\widetilde{B_{0}}
, we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{B_{0}}}(X,Y)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(X,\Sigma Y).

Let
M
be an object in
\mathrm{mod}\widetilde{B_{0}}
. By the
(n+1)
-Calabi–Yau property of
\mathrm{pvd}(B)
and the above isomorphism, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{B_{0}}}(M,\widetilde{B_{0}})&\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(M,\Sigma\widetilde{B_{0}})\\
&\simeq D\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\widetilde{B_{0}},\Sigma^{n}M).\end{split}

If
n=1
, we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\widetilde{B_{0}},\Sigma M)\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{B_{0}}}(\widetilde{B_{0}},\Sigma M)=0
. Suppose that
n>1
. There exists a canonical triangle in
{\mathcal{D}}(B)

\tau_{\leqslant-1}B\rightarrow B\rightarrow\widetilde{B_{0}}\rightarrow\Sigma\tau_{\leqq-1}B.

Since the spaces
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(B,\Sigma^{n}M)
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\Sigma B,\Sigma^{n}M)
vanish, it follows that

\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\widetilde{B_{0}},\Sigma^{n}M)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\tau_{\leqslant-1}B,\Sigma^{n-1}M).

By the previous discussion, the
i
-th homology
H^{i}(B)
vanishes for
i=-1,\ldots,-n+1
. We see that
\tau_{\leqslant-1}B
lies
{\mathcal{D}}(B)_{\leqslant-n}
. Thus, the space
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathcal{D}}(B)}(\tau_{\leqslant-1}B,\Sigma^{n-1}M)
vanishes and then
\mathrm{Ext}_{B_{0}}^{1}(M,\widetilde{B_{0}})
vanishes. It follows that
\widetilde{B_{0}}
is injective.

∎

By Propositions
5.49
,
6.2
,
8.14
and the above Lemma which was first proved in
[
27
, Corollary 3.4]
, we get the following Theorem.

Theorem 8.17
.

[
27
, Theorem 1.1]

Consider the relative cluster category
\mathcal{C}_{n+1}(A,B)
associated with

f\colon B=\bm{\Pi}_{n+1}(B_{0})\longrightarrow A=\bm{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0}).

a)

The Higgs category
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{n+1}(A,B)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{mod}(\widetilde{B_{0}})
and the image of
A
in
\mathcal{H}
is an
(n+1)
-cluster-tilting object.

b)

We have a triangle equivalence
\underline{\mathrm{mod}}(\widetilde{B_{0}})\cong\mathcal{C}_{n+1}(A_{0}/A_{0}eA_{0})
, where
e=f_{0}(\boldmath{1}_{B_{0}})
.

Remark 8.18
.

Above, we have used a different method to reprove Iyama–Oppermann’s results in
[
27
]
. Notice that the algebra
H^{0}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0}))
, which is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n+2}(A_{0},B_{0})
, is isomorphic to the (non stable) endomorphism algebra of the
(n+1)
-cluster-tilting object
T
given by the image of
A
in
{\mathcal{H}}
. This algebra does not appear explicitly in
[
27
]
.

Example 8.19
.

Let
Q
be a Dynkin quiver and let
\mathrm{Aus}(kQ)
be the Auslander algebra of the path algebra
kQ
. We consider the following canonical dg inclusion

f_{0}\colon kQ\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Aus}(kQ),

which maps each vertex
i
to the corresponding projective module
P_{i}=e_{i}kQ
.

We know that
\mathrm{gl.dim}(kQ)=1
and
\mathrm{gl.dim}(\mathrm{Aus}(kQ))\leqslant 2
. Moreover, the homotopy cofiber of
f
is the stable Auslander algebra
\underline{\mathrm{Aus}}(kQ)=\mathrm{Aus}(kQ)/\langle e\rangle
, where
e=f(\boldmath{1}_{kQ})
(see Proposition
8.13
).

Applying the relative 3-Calabi–Yau completion to the functor
f_{0}
, we get the following dg functor
f
which has a canonical left 3-Calabi–Yau structure and
\bm{\Pi}_{3}(\mathrm{Aus}(kQ),kQ)
is concentrated in degree 0

f\colon\bm{\Pi}_{2}(kQ)\rightarrow\bm{\Pi}_{3}(\mathrm{Aus}(kQ),kQ).

On the level of
H^{0}
, we get a fully faithful inclusion

H^{0}(f)\colon\widetilde{kQ}\hookrightarrow\bm{\Pi}_{3}(\mathrm{Aus}(kQ),kQ),

where
\widetilde{kQ}
is the preprojective algebra of
Q
and hence is self-injective. So the Higgs category
\mathcal{H}=\mathrm{gpr}(\widetilde{kQ})
is equivalent to
\mathrm{mod}(\widetilde{kQ})
.
By Theorem
8.17
, we get a triangle equivalence

\underline{\mathrm{mod}}(\widetilde{kQ})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{C}_{\underline{\mathrm{Aus}}(kQ)}=\mathrm{per}\bm{\Pi}_{3}(\underline{\mathrm{Aus}}(kQ))/\mathrm{pvd}(\bm{\Pi}_{3}(\underline{\mathrm{Aus}}(kQ))).

Thus, we have reproved that
\mathrm{mod}(\widetilde{kQ})
contains a canonical cluster-tilting object (see
[
15
]
) and that
\underline{\mathrm{mod}}(\widetilde{kQ})
is triangle equivalent to
\mathcal{C}_{\underline{\mathrm{Aus}}(kQ)}
(see
[
4
]
).
